                                                 PREFACE

         Birth and Deaths are the two most important vital events that define life of an individual. Not
only do they describe the legal existence of an individual, registration of these events is a primary
source of vital data of the population to which they belong. Civil Registration is a continuous,
permanent, compulsory recording of the occurrence and characteristics of vital events, like births,
deaths and still births. These vital statistics are invaluable for planning, monitoring and evaluating
various programmes related to primary health care, family planning, maternal and child health,
education etc. Information collected through the registration process about vital events provides
useful and important statistics.

2. This issue of the Report on `Vital Statistics of India based on the Civil Registration System' for
the year 2023 provides an overview of the working of the Civil Registration System (CRS) in the
country and presents a compilation of data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and still births
with rural-urban & sex-wise break-up and sex ratio at birth based on Civil Registration Records.
Based on the Annual Reports of the States/UTs, the report for the year 2023 containing key
information of events pertaining to the period 1st January 2023 to 31st December 2023 has been
brought out for the convenience of users.

3. The publication is divided into two parts. Part-I provides a brief review of the Civil Registration
System and contains a summary analysis of data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and sex
ratio at birth based on registered births at the State level. Part-II consists of the main tables which
provide State and District-wise data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and still births with
rural-urban & sex-wise break-up.

4. The data source for the report is the statutory Annual Statistical Report prepared by the Chief
Registrars of Births & Deaths of States/UTs. I take this opportunity to express my sincere gratitude
to all the Chief Registrars of Births & Deaths of various States/UTs for their kind cooperation in
providing the requisite information. I am thankful to each one of them for their invaluable and
continuing cooperation.

5. I would like to take this opportunity to mention that the Registration of Births & Deaths Act
(RBD Act), 1969 has been amended for the first time since its inception, through the Registration of
Births & Deaths Act, 1969(Amended in 2023) (No. 20 of 2023) with a view to accommodate changes
and new developments in the society during the last five decades. Various provisions of the Act have
been suitably amended to enhance the convenience of general public in getting the events registered.
I am hopeful that more dynamic implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act 1969(amended in
2023) shall help reduce the time gap and establish this as a reliable source of information on Birth
and Death registration in the country.

6. The report has been prepared by the CRS Section of this office. I would like to place on record
my appreciation for the efforts put in by all the officers and staff working in the CRS Section of the
Office of the Registrar General and the Officers/Staff connected with the Civil Registration System in
the States/UTs.

New Delhi                              Mritunjay Kumar Narayan
October, 2025                          Registrar General of India

         The information published in this report has been obtained from the Offices of Chief
Registrar of Births & Deaths of States/UTs or their sub-ordinate offices. The Office of
Registrar General, India (ORGI) has only compiled and collated the data and presented it in

for the authenticity of the information. All efforts have been made to make this report
foolproof. However, any discrepancy observed in this report may be brought to the notice
of this Office.

                   .Overview of Civil Registration System (CRS)

1. Registration of birth is a right of every child and is the first step towards establishing
         her/his legal identity.

2. It is compulsory to report births and deaths to the Registrar of Births and Deaths under the
         Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (as amended in 2023).

3. Births and deaths are registered only at the place of their occurrence.

4. Head of the household is responsible to report births and deaths that occurred in the house
         to the Registrar of Births and Deaths of the concerned area.

5. It is the responsibility of the medical officer in-charge or any person authorised by her/him
         to report the births and deaths that occur in hospitals, maternity or nursing homes, health
         centres or other like institutions to the Registrar of Births and Deaths of the concerned
         area.

6. Provision for use of the Birth Certificate as a single document to prove the date and place
         of birth of a person born on or after the date of commencement of the Registration of Births
         and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2023, i.e. 1st October, 2023.

7. Births and deaths are reported in the prescribed forms - Form 1 for live birth; Form 2 for
         death and Form 3 for still birth.

8. Midwife or any other medical or health attendant who has attended or was present at the
         time of birth or death are duty bound to notify the concerned Registrar about the
         occurrence of the event.

9. Keeper or the owner of a place set apart for the disposal of the dead bodies or any person
         required by a local authority to be present at such place are required to notify the
         concerned Registrar about the occurrence of the death.

10. The Registrar may either orally or in writing require any person to furnish any information
         within his knowledge in connection with a birth or death in the locality within which such
         person resides and that person shall be bound to comply with such requisition.

11. Births and deaths are to be reported within 21 days of occurrence of the event.

12. The birth and death certificates are issued free of charge by the concerned Registrar for
         events reported within 21 days.

13. Registrar to issue Birth or Death certificate, electronically or otherwise as soon as the
         registration is completed but not later than seven days.

14. The birth/death reported after 21 days of its occurrence but within 30 days can be
         registered on payment of the prescribed late fee.

                                                                            xii
15. The birth/death reported after 30 days but within one year of its occurrence can be
         registered with the written permission of the District Registrar or such other authority and
         on production of self-attested documents and on payment of such fee as prescribed.

16. Births and deaths reported after one year of its occurrence shall be registered only on an
         order of the District Magistrate or Sub-Divisional Magistrate or by an Executive Magistrate
         authorised by the District having jurisdiction over the area where birth or death has taken
         place after verifying the correctness of birth and death and on payment of the prescribed
         fee.

17. A birth can be registered without the name of the child.

18. Name of the child can be entered in the birth certificate within 12 months without any fee
         and after one year but within 15 years of registration on payment of prescribed fee and by
         approaching the concerned Registrar.

19. Error(s), if any, in the particulars of birth or death records can be corrected as per the
         prescribed procedure under Section 15 of the Act.

20. Any entry in the birth or death records made fraudulently or improperly, can be cancelled
         by the concerned Registrar.

21. Under Section 17 of the RBD Act, any person can cause a search in the birth or death
         registers for any entry and obtain a certified extract. Such extracts shall be admissible as
         evidence of birth or death to which the entry relates.

22. All Registrars are required to send a monthly return regarding entries of births and deaths
         in the register kept by them. to the concerned Addl. District Registrar/District Registrar or
         Chief Registrar.

23. It is mandatory for all medical institutions to provide a certificate as to the cause of death
         to the Registrar and a copy of the same to the nearest relative

24. Appointment of Special "Sub-Registrars" in the event of disaster or epidemic for speedy
         registration of deaths and issue of certificates.

25. The birth of an Indian Citizen, occurred outside the country, can also be registered in India
         within sixty days of return of the parents at the place where they intend to settle. After this
         period, it could be registered under delayed registration provisions of the Act.

26. There is no provision to register death of an Indian Citizen that has occurred outside the
         country.

27. Non-reporting of births and deaths, giving false information for inclusion in the register,
         refusing to put signature in the reporting form/register and non-registration of events
         reported to the Registrar is punishable under the Act.

28. Registrars and Sub-Registrars are deemed to be the public servants.

                                                                            xiii
29. Registration of births and deaths made prior to implementation of the RBD Act, 1969
         (amended in 2023) is deemed to have been done under the said Act.

30. On adoption of a child, the name of the adoptive parents and a new name for the child can
         be entered in the birth register.

31. Provision for redressal of grievances of general public aggrieved by any action or order of
         the Registrar or District Registrar.

32. Creation of National and State level databases of registered births and deaths which would
         help in updating other databases resulting in efficient and transparent delivery of public
         services and social benefits.
                                                         

                                                                            xiv
                                      EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

         Civil Registration System (CRS) in India is the unified process of continuous, permanent,
compulsory, and universal recording of vital events (births, deaths, stillbirths) and characteristics
thereof. The Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (as amended in 2023) provides for the
compulsory registration of births and deaths. Civil Registration records are the best source of Vital
Statistics. For the individual, records emanating from CRS provides her/his legal identity and
access to the rights of a citizen including entitlements (social benefits provided by the
Government). For the country, the requirement of a complete Civil Registration System is a must
as it has important administrative and statistical uses. The data generated through a complete and
up to date CRS is essential for socio-economic planning and to evaluate the effectiveness of various
social sector programmes. They also serve as the cornerstone of the public health system. The
Annual Report on Vital Statistics of India based on Civil Registration System for 2023 has been
brought out on the basis of reports provided by the Chief Registrar of Births and Deaths of
respective States/Union Territories. The main features of the Annual Report are summarized
below:

Birth Registration:

    · The number of registered births has decreased to 252.1 lakhs in 2023 from 254.4 lakhs in 2022.
         (Statement 9, page 41; Chart 1, page 42)

    · Out of the total registered births, the share of male and female is 52.4% and 47.6% respectively.
         (Statement 16, page 51; Chart 6, page 51).

    · The share of births registered in rural and urban is 41.7% and 58.3% respectively. (Statement
         18, page 53; Chart 10 page 54).

    · The share of institutional births to total registered births is 74.7 %. However, this does not
         include information from Sikkim (Chart 14, page 57).

    · The level of registration of births for the year 2023 stood at 98.4%.(Statement 23, page 61;
         Chart 16 page 59).

    · 21 States/UTs have achieved 100% level of registration of births in 2023. (Chart 17, page 60).
    · 13 (population of more than 1 Crore as per Census 2011) out of 19 major States namely

         Haryana, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Rajasthan, Telangana, Uttarakhand, West
         Bengal, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Punjab, Andhra Pradesh and Gujarat have crossed 90% level of
         registration of births. (Statement 25, page 64)
    · In case of registration of births within the prescribed time period of 21 days, 11 States/UTs
         namely Gujarat, Puducherry, Chandigarh, D & N Haveli and Daman & Diu, Tamil Nadu,

                                                                            xv
         Lakshadweep, A & N Islands, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Goa, and Punjab have achieved more
         than 90% registration of births. (Statement 13, page 46)
    · 5 States i.e. Odisha, Mizoram, Maharashtra, Chhattisgarh, and Andhra Pradesh have achieved
         more than 80% to less than or equal to 90% registration of births within 21 days. (Statement
         13, page 46)
    · Further, 14 States/UTs viz. Assam, Delhi, Madhya Pradesh, Tripura, Telangana, Kerala,
         Karnataka, Bihar, Rajasthan, Jammu & Kashmir, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Meghalaya, and Uttar
         Pradesh are in the category of more than 50% to less than or equal to 80% in registering within
         the stipulated time period of 21 days and the remaining 5 States namely Uttarakhand, Manipur,
         Ladakh, Arunachal Pradesh, and Nagaland are under the category of less than or equal to 50%
         in completing the birth registration within the time limit of 21 days.(Statement 13, page 46).
         Sikkim has submitted only partial data and therefore not considered.
    · Highest Sex Ratio at Birth (SRB) based on Registered events has been reported by Arunachal
         Pradesh (1085), followed by Nagaland (1007), Goa (973), Ladakh & Tripura (972), and Kerala
         (967). (Statement 26, Page 66; Chart 20, page 67).
    · Lowest SRB has been reported by Jharkhand (899) followed by Bihar (900) and Telangana
         (906). (Statement 26, Page 66; Chart 20, page 67)

 Still Birth Registration:

    · The number of registered still births has decreased from 1.15 lakhs in 2022 to 1.01 lakhs in
         2023 (Statement 22, page 58)

    · Share of registered still births in rural and urban area is 35.7% and 64.3% respectively
         (Statement 22, page 58).

Death Registration:

    · The number of registered deaths has increased slightly from 86.5 lakhs in 2022 to 86.6 lakhs
         in 2023. (Statement 9, page 41; Chart 2, page 42)

    · Out of the total registered deaths, the share of male and female is 60.6% and 39.4%
         respectively. (Statement 16, page 51; Chart 7, page 51).

    · The share of deaths registered in rural and urban is 57.7% and 42.3% respectively. (Statement
         18, page 53; Chart 11 page 54).

    · The share of Institutional deaths in total registered deaths is 24.0%. However, this does not
         include information from Sikkim (Chart 15, page 57).

    · The level of registration of death is 97.2% for the year 2023. (Statement 24, page 63; Chart 16
         page 59).

                                                                            xvi
· 19 States/UTs have achieved 100% level of registration of deaths in 2023. (Chart 18, page 62).
· 13 major States out of 19 have crossed 90% level of registration of deaths. These are Andhra

    Pradesh, Gujarat, Haryana, Karnataka, Kerala, Maharashtra, Odisha, Punjab, Tamil Nadu, West
    Bengal, Telangana, Rajasthan, and Chhattisgarh. (Statement 25, page 64)
· In case of registration of deaths within the prescribed time period of 21 days, 9 States/UTs
    namely Chandigarh, Punjab, Lakshadweep, D & N Haveli and Daman & Diu, Haryana, Mizoram,
    Puducherry, Himachal Pradesh, and A & N Islands have achieved more than 90% registration
    of deaths to the total deaths registered. (Statement 13, page 46)
· 8 States/UTs i.e. Gujarat, Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, Meghalaya, Maharashtra, Goa,
    Chhattisgarh, and Telangana have achieved More than 80% to less than or equal to 90%
    registration of deaths within 21 days. (Statement 13, page 46)
· Further, 15 States viz. Madhya Pradesh, Tripura, Odisha, , Rajasthan, West Bengal, Delhi , Uttar
    Pradesh, Assam, Karnataka, Kerala, Bihar, Jharkhand, Uttarakhand, and Ladakh are in the
    category of More than 50% to less than 80% in registering deaths within the stipulated time
    period of 21 days and remaining 3 states namely Manipur, Nagaland, and Arunachal Pradesh
    are under the category of less than or equal to 50% in completing the death registration within
    the time limit of 21 days. (Statement 13, page 46)
· In the case of registration of infant deaths, the share of the urban area is 85.7% compared to
    rural with only 14.3%. (Statement 20, page 57).

                                                     

                                                                      xvii
                                      GLOSSARY OF TERMS

Birth: Live birth or still birth.
Live birth: Complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of conception, irrespective of the
duration of pregnancy, which, after such expulsion or extraction, breathes or shows any other evidence of
life, and each product of such birth is considered live-born.
Still Birth: Foetal death where a product of conception has attained at least the prescribed period of
gestation.
Death: Permanent disappearance of all evidence of the life at any time after live birth has taken place.
Infant Death: Death of a child less than one year old.
Level of Registration: The level of registration, defined as the percentage of registered births/deaths to
the births/deaths estimated through SRS, determines the performance level of a State / Union territory
with regard to functioning of Civil Registration System.

                                                                           
                             () =        × 100

                                    =  -   ×  

Mid-year Population: Population at the mid-point of the year, i.e., 1st July of the year provided by
population projections based on 2011. (Source: Population Projections for India and States 2011­2036,
Report of the Technical Group on Population Projections constituted by National Commission on
Population, July. 2020)

of Registered Birth

                                               2
                                                    CHAPTER ­ I

                          CIVIL REGISTRATION SYSTEM - AN INTRODUCTION

1.1 The requirement for a complete and up to date Vital Statistical System to yield reliable
data on vital events hardly needs any emphasis. These data are essential for socio-economic
planning and development and also to evaluate the effective implementation of various public
schemes and programs. The main sources of data on vital events in India are Civil Registration
System (CRS), Sample Registration System (SRS) and Population Census. Though the Population
Census is the main source of information on population and its characteristics; however, being
a decennial exercise, it does not provide the measure of changes in population from year to year.
The measures of fertility and mortality derived from Census are centered on the midpoint of the
decade and as such do not provide yearly change in the population. Of the other two sources of
vital statistics for getting the vital rates on a regular basis, the SRS has been designed to provide
reliable estimates at National and State level. Robust estimates of vital rates at District level on
an annual basis through sample survey are difficult to obtain on account of prohibitively large
sample size and variety of resultant factors such as controlling of non-sampling errors etc. CRS
is thus the only source for providing vital rates at district level. Rather, a complete CRS can
provide these rates at lower administrative levels as well.

1.2 The Civil Registration System (CRS) may be defined as a unified process of continuous,
permanent, compulsory and universal recording of the vital events and characteristics thereof,
as per legal requirements in the country. In India, the Civil Registration System covers
registration of births and deaths. Registration of marriages and divorce are not covered under
the CRS in India. Civil Registration System provides the best source of information on the vital
rates at all levels. Ensuring cent percent coverage of vital events by registration machinery can
help in getting the critical vital statistics at all administrative levels, important for planning and
research. Therefore, emphasis should also be given to the proper monitoring of the activities
under the system. Civil Registration System is considered to be complete if it covers 100 percent
births and deaths taking place within a country or area and birth and death certificates are issued
to all registered events.

1.3 The registration records are primarily useful for their value as legal documents and
secondarily as the source of vital statistics. Civil registration as a source of vital statistics is
relatively less expensive, as the statistics generated from the registration records are the by-
products of an essential administrative process. The registration records can also generate
current and continuous statistics, which can be more accurate than any other method of data
collection apart from being a continuous source of such data. However, the accuracy of the vital
statistics obtained through the registration system depends much on the completeness and
promptness with which events are registered and the correctness of the information in the
registration records. The improvement in the system of registration of births and deaths is
critically dependent on the importance given by the respective States and District
administrations to this work and regular monitoring by them. Besides, the level of awareness

                                                                            3
among the general public about the utility of birth or death certificate is also equally and crucially
important.

1.4 Vital statistics obtained through registration records at different administrative levels
enable estimation of the size, demographic structure and geographical distribution of the
population except for migration. Vital statistics also enables the projections for coming years on
the basis of probable trends of fertility and mortality as derived from these vital statistics and
their interactions with social, economic and other demographic factors. The vital statistics data
generated through an effective Civil Registration System is of immense use for formulation of
various development and welfare programmes/ schemes and increases their effectiveness at
State and District level. The Civil Registration data acquires paramount importance in the wake
of 73rd and 74th amendments to the constitution of India, as it can provide data at local level for
micro level planning, monitoring and evaluation of schemes. At national level, the vital statistics
data generated through Civil Registration is also quite useful for medical research and in the
study of sex ratio, mortality rates and also in the study of causes of deaths.

Registration of births and deaths in India

1.5 In India, the registration of births and deaths is mandatory under provisions of the
Registration of Births and Deaths (RBD) Act, 1969(18 of 1969. The Act is administered by the
Office of the Registrar General of India (ORGI) and implemented by States/UTs. The Chief
Registrar is the Chief executive authority in the State for carrying into execution the provisions
of the Act and the rules and orders made thereunder.

1.6 Provision for registration of births and deaths and the mechanism thereof had been in
existence in India for more than a century. Earlier, the work of registration of birth and death
was carried out under various laws and byelaws existing at that time in different parts of the
country. The need for improvement in the vital statistics system was felt for long and, therefore,
after detailed deliberations at various levels, the Registration of Births & Deaths Act, 1969 was
enacted providing compulsory registration of births and deaths in India. Since then, this Act has
been enforced uniformly across all the States and Union Territories (UTs). This Act provides for
mandatory registration of births and deaths and publication of statistical reports annually based
on the civil registration data. The enactment of this Act has provided a common framework for
putting in place an effective system of registration of births and deaths in the country.

1.7 In order to simplify and speed up the process of registration and to bring in uniformity in
birth and death registration formats, the registration system was revamped in the year 2000.
Under the revamped system, the procedure for registration of births and deaths was also
modified to some extent and the forms were redesigned. The revamped system became
operational in 2000 in all States/Union Territories except Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, Gujarat,
Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Odisha, Punjab, Uttarakhand, and Uttar Pradesh. It was
implemented in Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, and Odisha in 2001, Haryana in 2002, Himachal
Pradesh, Uttarakhand, and Uttar Pradesh in 2003, Gujarat and Punjab in 2004, and Jharkhand in
2009.

                                                                            4
1.8 The Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 has been amended through the
Registration of Births and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2023 (No. 20 of 2023) and published in the
Gazette of India on 11th August, 2023(Annexure-A). The provisions of the amended Act have
come into force with effect from 1st day of October, 2023 (Gazette of India, No. 3896 dated 13th
September, 2023) (Annexure -B). The highlights of amendment may be seen in the following box:

               Major Highlights of Registration of Births and Deaths
                                (Amendment) Act, 2023

     (i) Insertion of provisions for digital registration and electronic delivery of certificate of births and
            deaths for the benefit of public at large;

     (ii) Creation of National and State level databases of registered births and deaths which would help in
            updating other databases resulting in efficient and transparent delivery of public services and social
            benefits;

     (iii) Provision for use of the Birth Certificate as a single document to prove the date and place of birth of
            a person born on or after the date of commencement of the Registration of Births and Deaths
            (Amendment) Act, 2023, i.e. 1st October, 2023, for admission to an educational institution, issuance
            of a driving license, preparation of a voter list, registration of a marriage, appointment to a post in
            Central Government or State Government or a local body or public sector undertaking or in any
            statutory or autonomous body under the Central Government or State Government, issuance of a
            passport, issuance of an Aadhaar number and any other purpose as may be determined by the
            Central Government in order to enhance the public convenience and to avoid multiplicity of
            documents to prove date and place of birth in the country;

     (iv) Changing the ordering authority from Magistrate of the first class or Presidency Magistrate to
            District Magistrate or Sub-Divisional Magistrate or an Executive Magistrate authorized by the
            District Magistrate in the case of delayed information of any birth or death to the Registrar after one
            year of its occurrence and submission of self-attested document instead of an affidavit made before
            a Notary Public in the case of delayed information of any birth or death to the Registrar after thirty
            days but within one year of its occurrence;

     (v) Facilitate registration process of adopted, orphan, abandoned, surrendered, surrogate child and
            child to a single parent or unwed mother;

     (vi) It is mandatory for all medical institutions to provide a certificate as to the cause of death to the
            Registrar and a copy of the same to the nearest relative;

     (vii) Appointment of Special "Sub-Registrars" in the event of disaster or epidemic for speedy registration
            of deaths and issue of certificates;

     (viii) Collection of Aadhaar numbers of parents and informant, if available, in case of birth registration;
     (ix) Provision for redressal of grievances of general public aggrieved by any action or order of the

            Registrar or District Registrar; and
     (x) Enhance the penalties provided in the Act.

                                                                            5
Statistical Reports
1.9 System of registration of births and deaths is constantly generating statistical
information at the local level. The information contained in the reporting forms is consolidated
by the Registrar for onward transmission to the Chief Registrar of Births & Deaths or to any
officer specified in the concerned State/UT. The Chief Registrar of the States/UTs, who are the
chief executive authorities for the activities on registration of Births and Deaths in the State/UT,
are mandated to submit to the State Government Annual Report on the Working of the Act in the
State/UT along with the Statistical Report referred under section 19(2) on registered births and
deaths for each year immediately following the year to which the report relates to. The Chief
Registrars are also required to send a copy of these Reports to the Registrar General of India,
Govt. of India.
1.10 Based on the Annual Reports prepared by the States/UTs, the Office of the Registrar
General of India, brings out an annual comprehensive report entitled "Vital Statistics of India
based on Civil Registration System". These reports have been brought out since 1958 (Before
the enactment of the Act, only statistical reports were brought out). However, some of the States
are not bringing out their Annual Statistical Reports in time, and this in turn, delays the
publication of the corresponding national report. It has also been observed that some States also
do not include all the prescribed tables in their reports thereby creating a limitation while
bringing out the national report. However, notwithstanding all these constraints, this report
provides vital indicators including the District-wise registered births and deaths along with
infant deaths with the sex-wise and rural-urban break-up of registered events for the year 2023.

                                                        

                                                                            6
                                      CHAPTER-II

                    ANNUAL REPORT FOR 2023 ON THE WORKING OF
           THE REGISTRATION OF BIRTHS AND DEATHS (RBD) ACT, 1969

                                        (AMENDED IN 2023)

2.1. Annual report on the working of the Registration of Births and Deaths

(RBD) Act, 1969(Amended in 2023): Under the provision of Section 4 (4) of the Act,

the Chief Registrars are required to submit an Annual Report on the working of the RBD
Act to the respective State Governments once approved, a copy of the same is sent to the
Office of Registrar General of India (ORGI). The ORGI compiles the reports received from
States/UTs and release the working of RBD Act report at National level. The aforesaid
Annual Report for the year 2023 has been received from all the States/UTs.
2.2. Registration Machinery: Section 3 to 7 of the Registration of Births and
Deaths (RBD)Act, 1969(amended in 2023) provides for the appointment of Civil
Registration System functionaries at all administrative and Jurisdictional levels viz.: -

(i)  National level:- Registrar General of India; (Section 3 of the RBD Act)

(ii) State level:- Chief Registrar; (Section 4 of the Act)

(iii) District level: - District Registrar/Additional District Registrar (Section 6 of the

     Act )

(iv) Local area level: - Registrar for a village, Panchayat, municipality, municipal

     Corporation, or other local bodies; (Section 7(1) of the Act), and;

(v) Sub-Registrar for specified area within the area under the jurisdiction of a

     Registrar (Section 7 (5) of the Act).

           The Registrar General of India (RGI) at Central level takes steps to coordinate
and unify the activities of Chief Registrars of all States/Union Territories and issues
general directions regarding registration of births and deaths under section 3(3) of the
Act. The work relating to Civil Registration in the office of the Registrar General of India
is handled by the Vital Statistics Division.

           The Chief Registrar, appointed by the State Government, is the chief executive
authority in the respective States/ UTs for implementing the provisions of RBD Act and
the Rules and orders made thereunder. The State Government also appoints District
Registrar of births and deaths for each revenue district. The actual work of registration

                                                                     7
of births and deaths in the field is carried out by the local Registrars, who are also
appointed by the State Government

           There is a wide variation in the registration machinery across the country,
details of which are given below: -

  The Chief Registrars of births and deaths in 17 States (Andhra Pradesh, Assam,

Gujarat, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Maharashtra, Manipur, Meghalaya, Odisha, Punjab,

Sikkim, Tamil Nadu, Telangana, Tripura, Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand and West Bengal)

and 4 Union Territories (A& N Islands, Chandigarh, Ladakh and Lakshadweep) are from

the Health Department, in 10 States (Arunachal Pradesh, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Goa,

Jharkhand, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Mizoram, Nagaland and Rajasthan) and 2

Union Territories (Delhi and Jammu & Kashmir) are from Planning, Economics &

Statistics Department, in 1 State (Kerala) from Local Self Government Department, in

UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli & Daman & Diu, Finance Secretary/Secretary (Planning &

Statistics) and in remaining 1 UT (Puducherry) it is with Local Administration

Department.

  The district level authorities in the States/UTs such as District Collectors/

District Magistrate, Additional Collector, Chief Medical Officers/ District Medical and

Health, Civil Surgeon, District Statistical/ Planning Officers, Deputy Commissioner,

Deputy Director (Panchayat) and Dy. Director (local administration) are appointed as

District Registrar.

  In rural areas, the registration work is entrusted to the officials of Panchayat

Department, Health Department, Revenue Department and Education Department.

                     8
While in urban areas, the Health Officer of Municipal Corporation / Municipalities etc.
or in absence of such a post, the Executive Officer of the town administration functions
as the Registrar of Births and Deaths (State and UT wise details of registration
functionaries are attached at Annexure-C).

2.3. Registration of Births and Deaths occurring to Indian Citizens abroad:
Under the provisions of Citizenship Act, 1955 the event of births and deaths to Indian
Citizens occurring abroad are registered by the respective Indian Missions/Consulates.
Under Rule 5 of Citizen's (Registration at Indian Consulates) Rules 1956, Consulates are
statutorily required to send the annual returns of births and deaths of Indian Citizens
to the office of the RGI. It has been observed that requisite returns are not furnished by
all Indian Missions/Consulates; such returns for the year 2023 have been received from
196 missions located in 150 countries, covering 62182 births and 10531 deaths. As can
be seen from the Statement 3 below, out of the total registered births at the various
missions during 2023 Mission/Consulate of Dubai located in UAE registered 12770 i.e.
about 20.5 per cent of the total births registered abroad, followed by Mission/Consulate
of London which is at 10 per cent. In case of Deaths, Mission/Consulate of Dubai located
in UAE registered 1687 i.e. 16 per cent of the total deaths registered abroad followed by
Mission/Consulate of Riyadh located in Saudi Arabia which is at 13.5 per cent.

2.1. Inter Departmental Coordination Committees (IDCC): In order to resolve
the operational problems and for proper implementation of the provisions of the RBD
Act and corresponding State Rules, coordination among the implementing departments
is essentially required. On the other hand, the work of registration of births and deaths
in most of the States/UTs is being done through the officials drawn from more than one
department of the State Government/Union Territory Administration. The need for
coordination between the stake holder departments is a pre-condition for effective
functioning of the system in each of the State. In view of above, the State level Inter-
Departmental Coordination Committees (IDCCs) have been constituted in States and
Union Territories. In most of the States / UTs, IDCC is chaired by the Principal
Secretary/Secretary of the Department from which the Chief Registrar belongs.

           In order to formulate the strategies for further improvements in Civil
Registration System and effective implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act in

                                    10
the respective State, it is desired to convene the meeting of such committee at least once
in a year. However, it has been observed that despite persuasion of this office from time
to time, such meetings have not been convened regularly by most of the States/ UTs.

  During 2023, such meetings were held in only 18 States/UTs namely Arunachal

Pradesh, Assam, Bihar, Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh, Karnataka, Kerala, Meghalaya,

Mizoram, Odisha, Punjab, Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh, West Bengal, Delhi,

Lakshadweep and Puducherry.      .

  Such Coordination Committees have also been set up at District and below

district level (i.e. at Mandal and Taluka level) in many States. However, the information

on convening the regular meeting of District Level Coordination Committees (DLCCs)

and below district level committee meetings has not been received from all the

States/UTs. The following information regarding district level and below district level

meetings during 2023 has been provided by the Chief Registrars in the respective

annual report of State/UT:-

registered respectively.

   Maharashtra: In the urban and rural areas of the State, the ORGI uniform CRS

portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in) is being used for registration of birth and death events from

2016 onwards.

   Manipur: Chief registrar issued several instructions/memorandum to all the

district registrar, registrar and medical officers for 100 percentage digitization of birth

& death in the state during the year 2023.

                                            16
  Meghalaya: Online registration process was implemented in 46 Registration

Units. Computerization of old Civil Registration Records has not been possible due to

lack of fund.

  Mizoram: Digitization of all the Civil Registration Records in Mizoram was

completed during 2019. These digitized records contain some minor errors and the

same are being rectified. Online Registration is not yet done at the Registration Unit

level. However, online submission to ORGI's CRS portal of all the reports of live Births,

still births and deaths are done at the respective ADR Offices. Moreover, all the records

are also kept in digitized form in the State specific software 'CRS Mizoram'.

  Nagaland: Births & Deaths data is compiled through computerization in every

district headquarter. Online registration of births and deaths through CRS ORGI portal

was launched in the state on 19th April 2022. Currently the Registration units in the

village/block level are functioning manually only but efforts are being made through

training for digitization of CRS in the rural areas. Online registration at district level not

functional due to connectivity issues. The State is taking concrete steps to upgrade the

ICT capability of the district headquarters for implementation of the ORGI uniform CRS

portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in) for online registration.

  Odisha: Online registration of births and deaths in 116 urban registration units

and all 314 Rural registration units started from 01.01.2021 in OBDRS portal, developed

by the State, and is continuing till date.

  Punjab: The e-Sewa application was introduced by the Department of

Governance Reforms (DoGR), Punjab in July, 2019 after revamping the earlier e-District

application. The services of issuance of birth and death certificate, addition of name of

child, correction in record and delayed registration of birth and death have been

included in the e-Sewa application and 535 Sewa Kendra's were established in the State.

All the birth and death certificates are now being issued electronically through eSewa

portal. If a citizen applies for a birth or death certificate and the relevant registration

record is still maintained in manual form then the related entry is digitized through

eSewa application and then a computer-generated certificate is issued.

  Rajasthan: The online registration of births and deaths through the web portal

`Pehchan' is being done since 01.01.2014 in the State. 100 percent computerization and

online registration is going on in the State.

  Sikkim: In the State, out of total 33 registration units, 19 were working online

since 2019 and since March 2023, all 14 remaining registrations units started

                                               17
functioning online on ORGI uniform CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in). Registration of

births and deaths record in physical form for the period 2015 to Dec, 2022 around

40,000 data records digitization process is going on with the fund provided by the state

government

  Tamil Nadu: The common CRS software (www.crstn.org) is implemented

uniformly throughout the State from 2018 onwards for registration of birth and death

and to issue/download the birth and death certificates. The digitization of legacy

records of registered births and deaths are carried out in the phased manner and the

data entry of about 3 crore data have been done and the process of transferring it to the

TNCRS portal is under process.

  Telangana: Civil registration records are fully digitalized in the State.

  Tripura: The work of online registration of births & deaths and reporting of

each event has been started through ORGI uniform CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in)

all over the State from 8th April 2016 onwards.

  Uttarakhand: The online registration of births & deaths is done in complete

State through ORGI uniform CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in). Computerization of old

registration record was done partially some districts due to shortage manpower in

others birth and death registration work.

  Uttar Pradesh: The registration of births and deaths is being done using ORGI

uniform CRS portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in) and computerized birth and death certificates

are issued by all registration units. Tablets have been provided to health units for

implementation of aadhaar based birth registration process for birth registration of

new born. Apart from this, birth and death registration data is link with various

beneficiaries schemes such as shramik kalyan yojana, Mukhya Mantri Kanya sumangla

yojana and Parivar Labh yojana through API based authentication.

  West Bengal: The State's own portal Janma Mrityu Tathya (JMT) has been used

since 5th May, 2022 and the use of ORGI Portal has been discontinued. CRS Portal & JMT

Portal have been integrated and almost all data registered in CRS Portal are available as

old legacy data in JMT Portal. During the year, "1427576" births and "662991" deaths

were registered using the State's own Software.

  Andaman & Nicobar Islands: The ORGI uniform CRS online software

(www.crsorgi.gov.in) has been implemented in all the registration units w.e.f. 01-01-

2016 and all the old birth and death records have been uploaded in CRS portal w.e.f.

Aug 1982.

                                           18
  Chandigarh: Registration units of UT are working on the CRS portal since

October 2015. The birth and death data from 1996 onwards are already available on the

portal. Additionally, the legacy data from 1958 to 1995 has been fully scanned and

digitized, and is ready to be uploaded to the CRS portal.

  Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: Registration of birth and deaths in

all the registration Centers in Rural as well as Urban area have been computerized and

online registration is being carried out.

  Delhi: All local bodies i.e. New Delhi Municipal Council (NDMC), Municipal

Corporation of Delhi (MCD) and Delhi Cantonment Board (DCB)have computerized the

birth & deaths registration system. All hospital are reporting the events online to the

concerned registrar (Births & Deaths). NIC has developed new software for Birth and

Death registration process, addition of name of child, correction in birth/ death records

in Municipal Corporation of Delhi. This has enabled a complete online system to

facilitate citizen to apply Birth and death registration/certificates from their home.

Birth/death certificates can also be downloaded by the Citizens from the websites of

concerned local bodies. In NDMC & DCB, almost all hospital are reporting the vital

events online. Soft copy of the CRS data is supplies along with MCCD to chief Registrar's

office by the local bodies for preparation of annual report.

  Jammu & Kashmir: Registration of Births & Deaths has been reported to carry

through CRS portal across all registration units in UT of J&K except Cantonment Boards

of Satwari (Jammu) and Badami Bagh (Srinagar).

  Ladakh: The online Registration system CRS started in UT ­ Ladakh from

December, 2022 onwards.

  Lakshadweep: All the registration units of Hospital/CHC/PHC are

computerized with support of UT of Lakshadweep Administration from 2008 onwards.

Now all Births, Deaths & still births are registered online by using the CRS uniform

software provided by the ORGI and therefore, online certificates are issued on time.

  Puducherry: All local bodies are issuing computerized birth and death

certificates for the event registered under the Act. In order to cater the needs of the

public who requires birth and death certificates of their ancestors, computerization of

birth and death record has been taken up for the period prior to 1979 in Puducherry

and Oulgaret Municipalities. The computerized birth records from 1930 and death

records from 1954 are available in Puducherry Municipality. In Oulgaret Municipality

birth records from 1920 and death records from 1979 are available.

                                           19
2.3. Penalties: There is a provision for imposing penalties on defaulting

institutions, and the individuals for non-reporting and for providing false information

and also on Registrars for negligence or refusal to register any birth or death without

any reasonable cause under section 23 of the RBD Act, 1969. Despite the aforesaid

provision, most of the State Governments are not imposing penalties on the defaulters

It has been found that very few States have reported the details on imposing the

penalties. The details of imposing penalties and prosecutions were not provided by

majority of States. However, few States have initiated actions in this regard, the details

of which are given below: -

  Andhra Pradesh: During the year 2023, show-cause notices were issued to the

Registrars by the District Registrars, where Births and Deaths events were registered

without proper field verification.

  Arunachal Pradesh: No penalties were imposed under section 23 of the RBD

Act, 1969 during 2023 on defaulting institutions, Registrars, and individuals for non-

registration /non-reporting and for negligence to register any birth/death.

  Assam: No prosecutions of CRS functionaries were done during the year 2023,

but some of the officials were called for explanation for irregular practices and

reminded not to delay the process.

  Chhattisgarh: Only in two cases penalties were imposed in the Year 2023.

  Haryana: All medical institutions in the state have been registered in the CRS

portal and are reporting the events online in time. There was no such institution in

knowledge which did not reporting births / deaths events in CRS portal. Also, some of

the birth and death registration services have been covered under Haryana Right to

Service Act, 2014. Time frame for each and every service has been fixed under the RTS

Act and regular monitoring is being done at senior level. Hence no delay in action at the

level of Registrar was observed. Therefore, no penalty was imposed on institution

during year 2023.

  Karnataka: No prosecution was launched during the year. Penalization was

avoided and improvement was attempted through educative, publicity/propaganda

and persuasive measures.

  Kerala: During 2023, the maximum numbers of prosecutions (46) were from

Palakkad Panchayat followed by Kasargode panchayat (40). The maximum number of

                                    20
offences 792 and 288 compounded in Malappuram and Kannur Panchayats
respectively. State wise details of Penalties imposed are given below in Statement 5:

  Statement 5: Details of Penalties imposed by States during the year 2023

         Tamil Nadu: Directions were issued under Section 4(4) to the connected heads

of the Registration of Births and Deaths Act is enforced to improve the registration

system. During the year Rs 8750 was collected through Penalties imposed.

         Uttarakhand: No incident of prosecution and compounding of offences has

taken place in the State. Improvement has been made by giving adequate publicity to

educate and sensitize general public through appeal to stay away from wrong doing and

legal tangle.

         Chandigarh: In the year 2023, the penalties were imposed under section 23 of

the RBD Act, 1969 on individuals for negligence to report birth or death.

         No Penalty Imposed: The States of Arunachal Pradesh, Assam, Goa, Gujarat,

         Bihar, Rajasthan, and Uttar Pradesh: The States of Bihar, Rajasthan and

Uttar Pradesh have not provided any information in the report on imposing penalties

for non-reporting/registration and for negligence or refusal to register the event.

2.4. Inspection of Registration units: Section 18 of the RBD Act, 1969 provides
for inspection of registration offices and records in order to improve the registration
system in qualitative as well as quantitative terms. The inspections are required to be

                                                                    21
done by the office of the Chief Registrar and District Registrars in each State/UT. Such
inspections could have been more meaningful, if organized and conducted frequently
within a regular time frame. However, as per the information provided by the States,
the inspections of registration centers were observed to be irregular and extremely
deficient in most of the States/UTs. The actual information in respect of number of units
inspected is not provided by majority of the States /UTs, the details of information
regarding inspection provided by some of the States/ UTs during the year are given

2.5. Training of Civil Registration Functionaries- Civil Registration functionaries

involved in registration of births and deaths at various levels in most of the States/UTs

belong to different departments. Almost all of these functionaries perform registration

work as an additional work along with other work assigned to them by their respective

department. In addition, due to frequent transfer of registration functionaries, there is

a need to impart training at regular intervals on RBD Act, 1969 (Amended in 2023) and

registration rules, procedures and practices being followed in the State/UT. As per the

information furnished by the States, the details of training programs organized in

various States during the year 2023 are given below:

  Andhra Pradesh: Virtual trainings were conducted for newly recruited/

promoted / transferred registrars and assisting staff in the entire state.

  Arunachal Pradesh: CRS /MCCD Training Programme was organized at

Directorate of Economics & Statistics (DES) - HQ for officials during 2023.

  Assam: Due to absence of fund in the State budget for implementation of RBD

Act, trainings could not be arranged during the year. However, with the support of

Director Census Operations, Assam and the concerned Joint Director of Health Services

trainings were conducted in a few Districts.

  Bihar: 12 Training programmes of officers and officials were organized at

district. Also, 106 trainings were conducted at block levels during the year, 2023 for

capacity building.

  Chhattisgarh: 128 training programmes on provisions of RBD Act and State

rules framed thereunder were organized for Civil Registration functionaries at rural and

urban areas and Registrar of institutional registration units.

  Goa: Although no formal trainings as such were conducted during the year

2023, regular interactions are continuously held with the Civil Registration

functionaries at the Village Panchayat and Municipal level along with the special birth

and death registration unit at Goa Medical College, Bambolim, in order to solve any

problem/difficulty faced by them in carrying out their day-to-day activities.

  Gujarat: During the year 2023, a module was developed for the Role

Assignment of Rural Registration units in e-Olakh application, power has been given to

                                              26
the District Registrars for the smooth functioning and training was also imparted for

this module at State level at Digital Platform for the associate stakeholders.

  Haryana: A meeting of Statistical Assistants is organized at state HQ every

month. In these meetings, besides collecting the monthly returns, participants were

trained on day-to-day functioning of the system. Their problems were also sorted out in

these monthly meetings.

  Himachal Pradesh: During the year 2023, 3 training programmes were

organized in District Kinnaur.

  Jharkhand: 24 District level and 264 block level training programmes were

organized during the year 2023. These programmes were attended by 1440 and 10581

participants respectively at District and block level.

  Karnataka: 125 trainings were conducted during 2023. District Statistical

Officers conducted training programs in all the Taluks and in Bruhath Bangalore

Mahanagara Palike to improve the Civil Registration System. Detailed procedures of

RBD Act and State Rules, Clarifications and discrepancies in filling up of reporting forms

and use of e-JanMa software were also discussed during training.

  Kerala: CRS training to all local registrars was conducted under the leadership

of Kerala Institute of Local Administration using the funds of the state government

during the year 2023. The staff of the hospital kiosks have been trained online with the

help of software under the guidance of Chief Registrar and Additional Chief Registrar to

ensure accuracy of information in birth and death registrations.

  Maharashtra: An Online training has been conducted for Registrars, Births

and Deaths. Besides this, two training programmes were conducted on the subject CRS

for newly appointed medical officers and statistical investigators during 2023.

  Manipur: Two days training progammes on digitization of birth and deaths

registration system for Civil Registration functionaries was organized during 2023. This

training programme was attended by Registrars/Sub Registrars.

  Meghalaya: During the year 2023, Following efforts were made on training:

  · Training was conducted by the Office of Census Operations, Meghalaya on

Online Registration of Births & Deaths using Office of the Registrar General, India

(ORGI) Portal, for CRS functionaries of 6 Registration Units.

  · Virtual Training was conducted by the Office of Census Operations, Meghalaya

on Online Registration of Births & Deaths using Office of the Registrar General, India

(ORGI) CRS Portal.

                                27
  · With IPA funding, Training of Trainers (2 CRS functionaries from each district)

on Civil Registration System (CRS) was conducted during 2023.

  · One-day training and testing of the Revamped CRS (ORGI) Portal was

conducted by the Office of Census Operations, Meghalaya for two Master Trainers.

  · With IPA funding, Hands-On Training was conducted by the two Master

Trainers for 2 CRS functionaries from each district on Revamped CRS (ORGI) Portal.

  Mizoram: During the year 2023, annual training of RBD, Act and Rules was

convened at all 11 district headquarters and at the same time general guidelines and

registration procedure including frequently asked questions (FAQ) in Mizo (local)

dialect were issued by office of the Additional Chief Registrar and the same was

distributed to all Registration Units.

  Nagaland: A one day Training of Trainers on Online Civil Registration System

was conducted in the DES to train the district staff as resource persons for imparting

training to Registrars of Births & Deaths in their respective districts.

  Punjab: The training of new officials and re-orientation courses for

registration functionaries are arranged at State headquarters to keep the functionaries

well versed with the latest clarifications and provisions of the Act. The participants are

also attending various courses at the Regional Health Statistics Training Centre SAS

Nagar and Health and Family Welfare Training Centre, Amritsar. The State Institute of

Health and Family Welfare Training, Mohali has also imparting trainings on the subject

of registration of births and deaths. Apart from this, Department of Governance Reforms

is also providing training to all the registration functionaries involved in the process of

various birth and death related services under E-Sewa regarding usage of this

application

  Rajasthan: 1 State level, 12 District level and 56 block level training

programmes were organized in the State for Civil Registration functionaries during the

year 2023. In all, 69 Training programmes were conducted in which 5145 participants

were trained.

  Tamil Nadu: Frequent orientation sessions were conducted at district and

state levels, including updates on the latest executive instructions and amendments to

the Registration of Births and Deaths Act(amendment), 2023. These sessions were held

via Zoom. Joint District Births and Deaths Registrars, Statistical Assistants, and other

CRS functionaries were trained on the highlights of the amended Act, with presentations

shared across districts.

  Uttarakhand: Training programme related to birth and death registration

information and online registration to the registrar in the district. Trainees were trained

on online registration and issuance of certificate through CRS portal.

  Uttar Pradesh: On the special request of the Director General of UP academy

registration work.

  West Bengal: Re-orientation training programmes are being conducted by the

  Chandigarh: One training programme was conducted for Civil Registration

for 2 days in Puducherry and Karaikal regions respectively and 1 day each in Mahe and

  No Training: 11 States/UTs namely, Madhya Pradesh, Sikkim, Telangana,

                                     29
programme on Civil Registration System during the year 2023. No information was
received from Odisha regarding Trainings conducted during the year 2023.

2.6. Publicity efforts for improving Registration of Births and Deaths: Lack of

awareness about the need and importance of registration among the general public and

lack of procedural knowledge among the registration functionaries are among

important reasons for low levels of registration in the country. In order to make the

public aware about the need and importance of registration, some of the State

Governments have undertaken various publicity measures through various means with

their own funds like printing of posters, cloth banners, pamphlets, leaflets on the

importance of registration of births and deaths. Radio talks were also arranged on the

importance of registration of births and deaths. In addition, advertisements were

published in leading newspapers. As per the details given by the States, following

publicity measures have been taken by them during the year: -

  Andhra Pradesh: Publicity on Civil Registration system has not been taken up

in the State due to lack of funds. However, the following steps were taken to create

awareness on registration of births and deaths during 2023: -

  · District Registrars were requested to instruct Registrars of Registration Units

  to ensure display of posters in all public places within the Registration Units.

  · Commissioner, Women & Child Department, was also requested to issue

  instructions to Project Directors to arrange for distribution and display of

  posters at Anganwadi Centers and to ensure Anganwadi workers notify Births

  / Deaths regularly to Village Secretaries.

  Arunachal Pradesh: Publicity measures were taken to create awareness

among the general public about the need, importance and benefits of births and deaths

registration via distribution of pamphlets through the students of various schools and

public on 26th January & 15th August 2023 and during the "Sarkar Apke Dwar Camps''

campaign through displaying Banners/Posters in the stall of Economics & Statistics

Department at General Ground, various district during the period 2023.

  Bihar: The Information and Public Relations department of State

Government carried out publicity on the importance of registration of births and deaths

by bringing out advertisements in daily newspapers from time to time.

  30
  Chhattisgarh: Publicity has been made through Pamphlets / Poster (4 Types

of A3 size posters) for birth and death registration. Video circulated on youtube (Hindi

and local language) for Birth and death registration.

  Goa: Information regarding various services provided by the Births and Deaths

Registration Units, fees charged for these services, timeline for delivery of services, etc.

have been displayed in all the offices of the Civil Registration functionaries for creating

awareness among the citizens.

  Gujarat: Registrars were sensitized for the early registration of birth and death

event during Monthly meetings held at the Taluka level.

  Himachal Pradesh: Posters for awareness on birth and death registration

were provided to district registrar to distribute among all the health and development

blocks during the year 2023.

  Karnataka: During the year 2023, wide publicity regarding importance of

registration of births & deaths were made through Newspapers and all publications

published by Directorate of Economics and Statistics. A full-fledged help desk with toll

free number 1800-425-6578 has been established for providing information on birth

and death registration in e-JanMa software and to resolve the grievances for the public.

  Kerala: In creating awareness among the public regarding the importance and

necessity of registration of vital events, various publicity measures were taken in the

year 2023 such as: -

  · Arranged publicity measures through Doordarshan and All India radio.

  · Permanent Notice Boards were exhibited in front of all registration units.

  · Social media like YouTube videos WhatsApp and departmental training

  programs are helping to popularize the importance of Civil Registration.

  · The details of services rendered, name of designated officer, time limit for

  registering births/deaths and the duties of the public under Civil Registration

  System are included in the `Citizen's Charter' published by the Gram Panchayats

  and Municipalities under the Kerala Panchayat Raj Act 1994 and Kerala

  Municipality Act 1994.

  Maharashtra: Official correspondence was made to all Registrars for proper

publicity in their jurisdiction to create awareness in general public. An article narrating

importance of registration and procedure of registration of births & deaths has also

been published in `Arogya Patrika' of Information, Education & Communication (IEC)

Bureau of Public Health Department.

                                                                    31
  Manipur: 5000 copies of leaflets on importance of births and deaths

registration were distributed in all the 16 districts in 2023.

  Mizoram: Adequate publicity has been made on the importance of Civil

Registration. Advertisements were often displayed in the daily newspapers and

periodicals in local language. Young Mizo Association (YMA), an NGO, and local

council/village council took up the responsibility of informing the public about the

importance of reporting vital events to the Registrar of births and deaths.

  Nagaland: Pamphlets, flyers, and posters were printed highlighting the

importance of online and timely registration of Births and Deaths and disseminated to

AHODs/Deputy Commissioner Offices/Medical Institutions/Municipal Councils,

Churches and Local Bodies in the State and it has also been shared in the local dailies

newspaper in Nagaland Post and Morung Express and also advertised in the digital

media Hornbill TV channel, Department of Information & Public Relations(DIPR) portal

for wide publicity.

  Odisha: Adequate publicity through Electronic media, All India Radio, Display

of Posters, Distribution of leaflets, screening of the films etc., has been done in the State

during the year on registration of births and deaths to popularize the Civil Registration

within community.

  Punjab: The following publicity measures were taken by the State: -

  · The District Registrar and Local Registrars have been instructed to display

boards or wall painting on the various provisions of the Act and procedure at important

places in their offices for public awareness.

  · Instructions issued to display publicity material in the various Exhibitions

organized by the department at block /village level.

  · Regular announcements on the importance of registration of births and deaths

have been made through public address system of Village Gurudwaras. Paramedical

staff also spread awareness in immunization camps during their routine visits.

  · Instructions were issued to District and Local Registrars to use CRS logo and

message of birth and death registration in regular correspondence.

  Rajasthan: Publicity on registration of birth and death were made through

Radio Jingles and FM Bands, through advertisements made in Newspapers, awareness

though Chaupal Programme of Doordarshan, through appeal made awareness on Births

and Deaths Registration by District administration during the year 2023.

  Tamil Nadu: The following publicity measures were taken by the State:

                                               32
  · Various IEC (Information, Education, and Communication) modes were used to

create awareness about the importance of birth and death registration. Campaigns

were conducted in educational institutions, hospitals, and public places.

  · Wide publicity was given to the extension of the time limit for name inclusion

in birth records until 31.12.2024. Advertisements were published in newspapers to

inform the public about the importance of registration and the extended time limit for

name inclusion.

  · Sample IEC materials were shared with district authorities for display in

delivery wards and public gathering areas. Registrars were instructed to display name

boards with their working hours in government institutions to inform the public about

their availability.

        Uttarakhand: Importance of Births & Deaths registration was discussed in

departmental and other meetings at district and block level and emphasis was laid on

informing the general public about the importance of birth and death registration.

Special emphasis was laid on 100% registration by creating public awareness through

ANM/Aaganwadi/ ASHA worker of the area. In addition, awareness among school

children on registration of birth and death was spread during other programs organized

in schools.

        Uttar Pradesh: An amount of 2.44 crore was allocated to all districts by state

to publicize among general public through hoardings/wall paintings/FM radios at the

district levels. Besides this, a short video film was made on the automatic birth

registration process through ManTra new born tracking application in government

hospitals. Apart from this information boards displaying name of registrar visiting

hours, room no. etc. were installed in government institutions to make general public

aware.

        West Bengal: Normal publicity efforts were taken during the year 2023.

        Andaman & Nicobar Islands: Intensive efforts were made by the Health

department for strengthening the effective internal mechanism of CRS in the Island

during the year 2023.

        Chandigarh: Public awareness advertisement boards have been installed in all

government and private hospitals and nursing homes across Chandigarh. This has

significantly contributed to awareness and compliance.

        Delhi: In the year 2023, during the field visits/surveys in the area/cluster

pamphlets containing the advantages of institutional deliveries related benefits under

                       33
the Government schemes, role of ASHA workers along with list of nearest hospitals were

distributed among the citizens by the O/o Chief Registrar (Births & Deaths). Awareness

was provided through pamphlets on how citizen can get birth certificate without any

hassle in case of hospital delivery. Publicity was also done through handbills, display

board and through website.

  Jammu & Kashmir: Information on CRS Portal was given to the general public

in various Public Darbars, Block Divas & Health Melas held across the UT of J&K. Special

camps were held at District/Blocks to sensitize the public about the importance of

Births & Deaths Registration, and various queries of the public raised in the camps were

addressed on the spot. During the Vikasit Bharat Sankalp Yatras, special awareness

drives were organized to highlight the importance of registration of Births & Deaths.

Banners/pamphlets depicting the details of registration process had been circulated

among the public including Chowkidar, AWW, Asha Worker, Auxiliary nursing

Midwives (ANM), Dias, Head Master of Govt. School, Care taker, in charge of cremation

grounds/ Burial ground through Block Development Officers.

  Ladakh: The Department has publicized through different media platforms

and highlighted the importance of registration of births and deaths.

 No Publicity: No publicity efforts have been made during the year 2023 in the

  States/UTs of Haryana, Madhya Pradesh, Meghalaya, Sikkim, Telangana, Tripura,

  Lakshadweep and Puducherry. No information is received from Assam, Dadra &

  Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu and Jharkhand

2.7. Issuance of birth and death extracts / certificates: The Registrar under the
provision of Section 12 of the RBD Act, 1969 are required to give an extract of
prescribed particulars from the register relating to birth or death, free of charge to the
person who gives the information under Section 8 and 9 of the said Act. In addition to
that, under Section 17 of the RBD Act, the extract relating to birth or death (any number
of copies) has also been issued on the payment of prescribed fee to the person asking
for it. The information in respect of issuance of extract/certificate is given in Statement
7.

           All of the States / UTs are not furnishing the desired information on issuance
of extract/ certificate under Section 12 and 17 of the Act. For the year 2023, 26 States/
UTs namely Andhra Pradesh, Arunachal Pradesh, Assam, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Goa,
Gujarat, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Karnataka, Kerala, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra,

                                                                    34
Mizoram, Nagaland, Odisha, Punjab, Rajasthan, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu, Uttarakhand, UT of
Andaman & Nicobar Islands, Delhi, Jammu & Kashmir, Lakshadweep, and Puducherry
have furnished the requisite information.
