2.8. Difficulties encounter in implementation of the Act: Some of the States have
furnished the details of problems facing in implementing the provision of RBD Act and
State Rules in the State/UT. The brief points given by the State Governments in their
annual report is summarized below: -

  Andhra Pradesh: No fresh sanction of posts of Statistical staff was made in
2023. The lack of budget under CRS and MCCD towards trainings, Publicity, inspection
and printing of forms affects the successful implementation of RBD Act in the State.
There are about 1.8 crore legacy events in UBD Portal of State. The data porting from
UBD to CRS software need to be completed.

  Arunachal Pradesh: Inadequate infrastructure is the major problem for
implementation of the Act. Further, due to shortage of vital statistics staff, lack of proper
communication, illiteracy among the villagers, villages being scattered in far flung areas,
etc. caused the issue of under reporting.

  Assam: Non-availability of CRS funds in State budget for implementation of
RBD Act, 1969. Due to this Trainings and inspections could not be carried out
adequately.

  Bihar: Guidance/ directions were issued to regional offices to resolve the
difficulties faced by them in implementation of the RBD Act during the year 2023.

  Goa: All the Births & Deaths records from 1971 onwards have been
computerized; there is a possibility of damage to the physical Birth/Death records as
the Registers containing these records are worn out over the years. Therefore, this
Office is planning to digitize these physical records during the year 2024, so that the
same can be stored in the digital format, before getting fully damaged.

  Gujarat: Some post such as Research Officer, Research Assistant and Statistical
Assistant are lying Vacant this may delay administrative/technical work.

                                                                    36
  Haryana: Births & Deaths Amended Act 2023 was implemented in the State.
Lack of Budget on Training for Civil Registration functionaries like Registrars need to
be trained regarding the new CRS Portal and the Amended Act.

  Himachal Pradesh: Local Registrars cum Panchayats Secretaries in concerned
Panchayats are not posting vital events online regularly. There is shortage of manpower
in some of the registration units across the State. Moreover, shortage of manpower and
budget in most of the health institutions across State and District Headquarter is also
affecting the routine working of online CRS.

  Jharkhand: Some difficulties are encountered in implementing the provisions
of RBD Act and Rules in respect of delayed registration. Difficulties in timely submission
of reports and supervision due to lack of Block Statistical Supervisor at block level.
Difficulties under uniform CRS software in obtaining affidavit by general public for
reporting events after 30 days of occurrence.

  Karnataka: Inadequacy of staff at all levels. Lack of administrative authority of
the Chief Registrar over registration functionaries.

  Kerala: Pre-occupation of Registrars and other functionaries with other
duties. Damage caused to very old registration records. Non-registration of large chunk
of infant and child deaths particularly in rural areas. Inclusion of details without care in
registration by the public and request for correction of particulars in the registers as
entered in other documents pertaining to the individuals. Lack of proper training are
some of the difficulties faced in the implementation of the Act.

  Madhya Pradesh: Vacant posts of Assistant Statistical Officer, Statistical Clerks
and Clerks in various Municipal Corporations and Municipalities; Lack of control over
Notifiers in the rural areas.

  Maharashtra: In rural area yet to perceive, the real need of the registration of
births and deaths. The Gram Sevaks who is Ex-officio Registrar of births and deaths, has
to cover many villages due to vacant posts. The situation may improve with increase by
appointing Gram sevak where posts are vacant as well as if possible appointing assistant
for day to day birth and death registration work.

  Mizoram: The geographic location of the State has affected the registration
process. Gap in communication and transportation facilities hampers timely reports
making it difficult to consistently maintain 100% level of reporting.

  Nagaland: Inadequate postal, transportation and communication facilities.
Lack of remuneration for CRS functionaries, delay in submission of reports by Registrar

                                                                    37
and low level of awareness and lack of Budget for CRS activities are some of major
difficulties in the State.

  Odisha: There is shortage of staff/manpower in the State. Vacancies of
Supervisory staffs at state headquarter, district level and sub-district level delay the
monitoring and supervisory work to great extent.

  Punjab: There is a shortage of Staff especially technical staff at all levels. More
and more technology is being involved in this work and various other softwares, being
used by different departments, are also being interlinked with the eSewa software.

  Rajasthan: Difficulties relating to implementation of Act in some cases such as
penalty, number of copies, adoption etc. were observed.

  Tamil Nadu: Non-availability of funds for sustaining the registration of births
and deaths through online facility in terms of software and hardware, inspection of
registration units, organizing training programme for CRS functionaries, Publicity on
CRS at State/Districts/Registration Unit levels etc.

  Telangana: Lack of Sub registrars and statistical staff in Municipalities. Large
number of vacant posts of Panchayats Secretaries.

  Uttarakhand: There is lack of registration information and the
computerization of old civil registration record/work is also not being done properly.
Lack of manpower, training, publicity, remote rural area and a lot of posts are lying
vacant in birth-death registration cadre of Health department are posing difficulties in
effective implementation of RBD Act.

  West Bengal: As such, there is no difficulty in the implementation of the Act at
the field level. However, there are 196 registration units functioning without Data entry
operators.

  Chandigarh: No major difficulty was faced during the implementation of the
Act in 2023. All hospitals in Chandigarh are complying with the provisions of the
Registration of Births and Deaths Act and are actively using the CRS portal for timely
registration of events.

  Puducherry: No difficulties encountered in implementing RBD Act since
compulsory Registration of vital events was prevalent during the French Rule.

                                       

                                                                    38
                                                  CHAPTER ­ III

                             A BRIEF ANALYSIS OF DATA ON REGISTERED
                                 BIRTHS, DEATHS AND INFANT DEATHS

3.1 Births and deaths are the two most important vital events in the life of an individual
and for society as a whole. Based on the data collected under the Civil Registration System
on registered births and deaths, this chapter presents the trend in the number of registered
events and other related indicators, at the State and National level.
3.2 The Registration Unit in the Civil Registration System is the most important entity
responsible for registering events such as births and deaths, issuing of certificates,
maintenance of CRS records and reporting to higher authorities. The number of
registration units for the year 2023 at the national level and the States/UTs with
rural/urban details may be seen in Statement 8.
3.3 The highest number of registration units i.e. 62780 were working in Uttar Pradesh,
followed by 43794 in Maharashtra, 33124 in Karnataka and 24550 in Madhya Pradesh.
Registration Units in 10 major States, namely, Uttar Pradesh, Maharashtra, Karnataka,
Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Tamil Nadu, Gujarat, Andhra Pradesh, Telangana and
Rajasthan accounts for about (252778 nos.) i.e. about 88.2% of the total registration units
of the country. In the case of urban areas, the highest number of registration units are in
Tamil Nadu(1985), followed by 1822 in Uttar Pradesh.
3.4 At the national level, about 3.4 percent of Registration Units are located in Urban
areas and 90.3% of registration Units are in Rural areas. The rural-urban bifurcation of
Registration Units of Chhattisgarh, which accounted for another 6.3% is not available.

3.6 The number of registered births has decreased from 254.4 lakhs in 2022 to 252.1 lakhs
in 2023 ­ a decrease of about 0.9%. The birth registration has increased in the year 2023 in
States/UTs of Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh, Jammu & Kashmir, West Bengal, Haryana, Gujarat,
Rajasthan, Chhattisgarh, Delhi, Andhra Pradesh, Manipur, Punjab, Himachal Pradesh, Mizoram,
D& N Haveli & Daman & Diu, Ladakh, Sikkim, A& N Islands and Chandigarh compared to 2022,
while in ten major states, namely, Uttar Pradesh, Bihar, Maharashtra, Assam, Telangana, Kerala,
Uttarakhand, Tamil Nadu, Odisha, and Karnataka, birth registration has decreased in the year
2023 compared to 2022.

3.9 Registration of Birth & Death within the prescribed time limit of 21 days
 3.9.1 Statement 13 shows the percentage of births and deaths registered within the prescribed
 time limit of 21 days. The registered births and deaths have been classified into four categories
 depending upon their period of registration. The four time periods considered for the purpose
 are: (i) within the prescribed time limit (up to 21 days), (ii) After 21 days but within 30 days,
 (iii) After 30 days but within 1 year, and (iv) More than 1 year. For 2023, Thirty-five (35)
 States/UTs have provided data on Births and Deaths Registration within the prescribed time
 limit (i.e. within 21 days).
 3.9.2 On the basis of data received from States/UTs the percentage of registration achieved
 out of the total registration undertaken in 2023 within the prescribed time limit (21 days) is as
 under:

3.9.3 It has been observed that 11 States/UTs have achieved more than 90% registration of
births within the prescribed time limit of 21 days. Further, 5 States are in the category of more
than 80 to less than or equal to 90 percent, 14 States/UTs are in the category of more than 50
to less than or equal to 80 percent, and the remaining 5 States/UTs are under the category of
less than or equal to 50 percent.

prescribed time limit of 21 days. Other 8 States/UTs fall in the category of more than 80 to less
than or equal to 90 percent, 15 States/UTs are in the category of more than 50 to less than or
equal to 80 percent and the remaining 3 States/UTs are under the category of less than or equal
to 50 percent in death registration within the prescribed time limit of 21 days.

3.9.5 Chart 5 shows the performance of major States in terms of percentage registration done
within prescribed time i.e. 21 days. In 7 States namely, Tamil Nadu, Haryana, Punjab,
Maharashtra, Gujarat, Chhattisgarh and Andhra Pradesh, the share of registered births and
deaths within 21 days is more than 80 percent in terms of both total registered births and
Deaths. The share of Madhya Pradesh is between 70 to 80 percent. In case of Odisha, the share
of timely registration is between 80-90 percent in case of births and is between 70 to 80 percent
in case of deaths. While in case of Telangana, the share of timely registration of births is
between 70-80% and corresponding shar of death is slightly higher in the range of 80-90%. In
case of Kerala and Assam, the share of within Time Birth Registration is between 70 to 80
percent while in case of death, it was slightly lower and was between 60 and 70 percent. In case

                                                                          47
of Bihar, Uttar Pradesh and Karnataka, the share of both Timely birth and death registration is
in the range of 60-70 percent.

3.16 Still Births

3.16.1 The World Health Organization (WHO) defines a still birth as a baby born with no signs
of life at or after 28 weeks' gestation. Still birth, also known as foetal death, has been defined as
`death prior to the complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of human
conception, irrespective of the duration of pregnancy and which is not an induced termination of
pregnancy. The death is indicated by the fact that after such expulsion or extraction, the fetus does
not breathe or show any other evidence of life, such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the
umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary muscles. Heartbeats are to be distinguished
from transient cardiac contractions; stillbirths are to be distinguished from fleeting respiratory
efforts or gasps.'

3.16.2 Statement 22 presents stillbirths registered by place of occurrence (Rural /Urban) of
the event for the year 2023. It may be observed that in total registered stillbirths, the share of
rural registered stillbirths is only 35.7%, while this share is about 64.3% in Urban Areas.
State/UT-wise registered stillbirths is depicted in Table 4 (Page No. 79).

             Statement 22: Still Births registered during 2023

Place of Occurrence  No. of Registered Still Births   % Share of Registered Still
                                                     Births by Place of Occurrence

      Rural                   35862                        35.7

      Urban                   64715                        64.3

      Total                   100577                       100.0

                                        58
Level of Registration (LOR)

3.17.1 The level of registration, defined as the percentage of registered births/deaths to the
births/deaths estimated through SRS, determines the performance of a State / Union Territory in
registration of births and deaths. In the absence of reliable estimates of number of births and deaths on
a year to year basis from any other source, the estimated number of events as revealed from SRS is used
to calculate the level of registration. The level of registration so calculated is very high for some of the
smaller States/Union Territories either due to the lower estimates of vital events than the actual and
due to the fact that SRS estimates of vital events are based on the place of usual residence while events
are registered at the place of occurrence irrespective of the place of residence.

3.17.2 Under this section, level of registration of Births & Deaths is based on revised mid-year
population projections based on census 2011. The levels of registration of births and deaths for India
and States/UTs for the period 2011 to 2019 and 2023 are shown in Statements 23 and 24 respectively.
The level of registration of births has increased to 98.4% in 2023 from 92.7% in 2019 i.e. in last 5 years
an increase of about 5.7%. Similarly, the level of registration of deaths has also increased to 97.2% in
2023 from 92.0% in 2019, showing an increase of 5.2% over the last 5 years. Chart 16 depicts the trend
in level of registration of births and deaths over the years. Due to continuous efforts of the States and
UTs the gap between Levels of Birth and Death Registration is narrowing down with a difference of
1.2% observed in 2023.

3.17.7 Statement 24 shows that the level of registration of deaths of the country and the States/UTs. In
2023, there were 19 States/UTs namely Andhra Pradesh, Goa, Gujarat, Haryana, Karnataka, Kerala,

followed by the Telangana, Himachal Pradesh, Rajasthan, Lakshadweep, and Chhattisgarh registering
more than 90 percent level of registration of deaths. 21 States/UTs have recorded their level of
registration of deaths higher than the National level of 97.2% while remaining 15 States/ UTs have their
level of registration of deaths below the national level. 8 major States namely Bihar, Jharkhand, Assam,
Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh and Rajasthan features among States that
have level of death registration below the National Level.

3.17.9    Andhra Pradesh, Gujarat, Haryana, Karnataka, Maharashtra, Punjab, Rajasthan, Tamil

Nadu, Telangana and West Bengal, are such major 10 States that are recording more than 90 percent of

registered births as well as deaths in 2023. Six Major States Bihar, Kerala, Uttar Pradesh, Odisha,

Chhattisgarh and Assam were placed in category 80-90% for Level of Birth Registration. None of the

Major States found to be having level of registration of births lower than 80 percent. In case of Level of

registration of deaths, 2 States namely Madhya Pradesh and Uttarakhand are in the category 80 to 90

percent while 4 States namely Uttar Pradesh, Assam, Jharkhand and Bihar are placed in the category

50-80 percent. No Major State is found to be having level of Death Registration below the 50 percent.

3.18 Sex Ratio at Birth

3.18.1 The Sex Ratio (Number of females per thousand males) at birth of registered events is an
important indicator to map the sex differential of the population at the beginning of their life.
The sex ratio at birth has been calculated after deducting the delayed registration of more than
1 year for the year 2023. Statement 26 shows Sex Ratio at Birth (SRB) based on registered events
for the years 2017-2023. Sex Ratio at Birth based on registered events for the year 2023 for All
India (excluding figures of Sikkim) stood at 928 females per thousand males.

3.20 Registered Births & Deaths of Indian Nationals in Indian Missions/Post abroad

3.20.1 Statement 29 presents the number of registered births and deaths during the year 2023
of Indian Nationals with Indian Mission/Post abroad. These events of Indian citizens occurring
abroad are registered by the respective Indian Consulates under the Citizenship Act, 1955. As
per the RBD Act Registrar General of India shall, subject to such rules as may be made by the
Central Government in this behalf, cause to be registered information as to births and deaths of
citizens of India outside India received by him under the rules relating to the registration of such
citizens at Indian Consulates made under the Citizenship Act, 1955 (57 of 1955), and every such
registration shall also be deemed to have been duly made under this Act.

                                  MINISTRY OF LAW AND JUSTICE
                                         (Legislative Department)

                                              New Delhi, the 11th August, 2023/Sravana 20, 1945 (Saka)

                    The following Act of Parliament received the assent of the President on the
             11th August, 2023 and is hereby published for general information:--

             THE REGISTRATION OF BIRTHS AND DEATHS (AMENDMENT)
                                                ACT, 2023

                         NO. 20 OF 2023

                                                                   [11th August, 2023.]

             An Act further to amend the Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969.

                    BE it enacted by Parliament in the Seventy-fourth Year of the Republic of India as
             follows:--

             1. (1) This Act may be called the Registration of Births and Deaths (Amendment) Short title and

             Act, 2023.                                                                                       commencement.

                    (2) It shall come into force on such date as the Central Government may, by notification
             in the Official Gazette, appoint.

18 of 1969.         2. Throughout the Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (hereinafter referred       Construction
             to as the principal Act), for the word "Registrar-General", wherever it occurs, the words        of references
             "Registrar General of India" shall be substituted.                                               of certain
                                                                                                              expressions by
                                                                                                              certain other
                                                                                                              expressions.

               2  THE GAZETTE OF INDIA EXTRAORDINARY  [PART II--

Amendment             3. In section 2 of the principal Act, in sub-section (1),--                                 18 of 2016.
of section 2.                                                                                                      2 of 2016.
                             (i) clause (a) shall be re-numbered as clause (ab) thereof, and before clause (ab)
Amendment             as so re-numbered, the following clauses shall be inserted, namely:--                       43 of 1950.
of section 3.
                                    `(a) "Aadhaar number" shall have the same meaning as assigned to it in
Amendment                    clause (a) of section 2 of the Aadhaar (Targeted Delivery of Financial and Other
of section 4.                Subsidies, Benefits and Services) Act, 2016;

                                    (aa) "adoption" shall have the same meaning as assigned to it in clause (2)
                             of section 2 of the Juvenile Justice (Care and Protection of Children) Act, 2015;';

                             (ii) clause (b) shall be re-numbered as clause (ba) thereof, and before clause (ba)
                      as so re-numbered, the following clause shall be inserted, namely:--

                                    `(b) "database" means the organised collection of data, generally stored
                             and accessed in electronic form from a computer network;'.

                      4. In section 3 of the principal Act,­­

                             (i) in the marginal heading, for the words "Registrar-General, India", the words
                      "Registrar General of India" shall be substituted;

                             (ii) in sub-section (1), for the words "Registrar-General, India", the words
                      "Registrar General of India" shall be substituted;

                             (iii) in sub-section (3), for the words "and submit", the words "and the database
                      of registered births and deaths and submit" shall be substituted;

                             (iv) after sub-section (3), the following sub-sections shall be inserted, namely:--

                                    "(4) The Registrar General of India shall maintain the database of
                             registered births and deaths at the National level and it shall be obligatory upon
                             the Chief Registrars and the Registrars to share the data of registered births and
                             deaths to such database.

                                    (5) Subject to the proviso to sub-section (1) of section 17 and with the
                             prior approval of the Central Government, the database of registered births and
                             deaths maintained under sub-section (4) may, on request, be made available to
                             the authorities dealing with the preparation or maintenance of database relating
                             to--

                                            (a) population register;
                                            (b) electoral rolls;

                                            (c) Aadhaar number;
                                            (d) ration card;

                                            (e) passport;
                                            (f) driving licence;

                                            (g) property registration; and
                                            (h) such other databases at the National level as may be notified,

                             and the authority shall inform the action taken, within such period as may be
                             notified from time to time, to the Central Government:

                                    Provided that the preparation or maintenance of database relating to
                             electoral rolls in clause (b) shall be without prejudice to the provisions of the
                             Representation of the People Act, 1950.".

                      5. In section 4 of the principal Act, after sub-section (4), the following sub-sections
               shall be inserted, namely:--

                             "(5) The Chief Registrar shall take steps to register births or deaths and maintain
                      a unified database of registered births and deaths at the State level by using the
                      portal as approved by the Registrar General of India and it shall be obligatory upon
                      the Registrars to share the data of registered births and deaths to such database.

              to the authority dealing with other databases at the State level and the authority shall
              inform action taken, within such period as may be notified from time to time, to the
              State Government:

                       his recent illness to the Registrar in such form as may be prescribed and provide a

                       history of illness, if any, to the person required under this Act to give information
                       concerning the death in such form as may be prescribed, and the person, on receipt of
                       the certificate, shall deliver the same to the Registrar at the time of giving information of
                       the death as required under this Act.".

          S.O. 4058(E).--In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (2) of section 1 of the Registration of
Births and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2023 (20 of 2023), the Central Government hereby appoints the 1st day of
October, 2023, as the date on which the provisions of the said Act shall come into force.
